  ### Verdict
  **Merge readiness:** Ready after the two Important fixes below. Module boundaries, user scoping and number consistency are sound, but two seams return errors that a client following the README will hit.
  
  I reviewed the diff in one pass, plus whole files for app.ts, modules/index.ts, shared/* and every scan, nutrition, meals, plan and profiles source file, both migrations, the seed generator, the catalog CSV, the README, plan Global Constraints and Review Focus, and spec sections 4 to 6 and 8. I ran `npm test -w backend` once: 19 files, 97 tests pass. I also ran `npm run lint -w backend` (read-only eslint and tsc): clean. I grepped the diff, `.env.example` and the uncommitted `.claude-logs/` for Gemini, JWT and Supabase keys, the project ref and the org id: none found. `backend/.env` and `.DS_Store` are ignored.
  
  ### Findings
  
  #### Critical (must fix before merge)
  None.
  
  #### Important (should fix before merge)
  
  **I1. Scanning or saving a meal before the profile exists returns an opaque 500, and the scan flow dead-ends.**
  - **Where:** `database/supabase/migrations/20261101000000_catalog_and_scan_log.sql:26` (`scan_log.user_id references profiles(id)`) and `20261030000000_initial_schema.sql:37` (`meals.user_id references profiles(id)`). No trigger creates a `profiles` row on sign-up.
  - **What happens:** A signed-in user with no profile row calls `POST /api/scan`. The photo is resized, then `recordScan` (`scan.service.ts:48`) hits a foreign-key violation and the user gets 500 `internal_error`. `POST /api/meals` fails the same way at `insertMeal`.
  - **Who it hurts:** any app build that lets the user scan or log before onboarding finishes (skipped onboarding, or a failed or slow PUT). This breaks the Global Constraint that "the scan flow never dead-ends". Review Focus 4 required exactly this case to be a 404, not a 500, for plan; scan and meals were not covered. The README does not mention it, and the e2e always ran PUT profile first.
  - **Smallest fix:** one migration with an `after insert on auth.users` trigger that inserts `profiles(id)`, plus a backfill: `insert into public.profiles(id) select id from auth.users on conflict do nothing`. All profile columns are already nullable, and `getProfile` already returns 404 "Set up your profile first" when the budget is null (`profiles.service.ts:26`), so plan keeps its 404 and scan and meals then work.
  - **Alternative:** map Postgres error 23503 to `NotFoundError('Set up your profile first')` in the scan and meals services.
  
  **I2. Following the README's save step fails the whole meal when the scan returned an `unknown` item.**
  - **Where:** `backend/README.md:342` tells the app to "Keep the scan's original `dish`, `portion` and `confidence` as the predicted values". The scan emits `dish: "unknown"` (`scan.service.ts:19-26, 69`).
  - **What happens:** `meals.service.ts:16` sends every non-null `predictedDish` to `nutrition.getDish`, which throws 404 "Unknown dish: unknown" (`nutrition.service.ts:15`). The whole meal is rejected. `meals.types.ts:3` says "null: unknown", but neither the README nor the schema says so.
  - **Who it hurts:** every user whose photo had an unrecognised dish, which Review Focus 1 calls an expected case, once the frontend dev implements the documented flow literally.
  - **Smallest fix:** in `meals.service.ts` `dishId`, treat `'unknown'` like `null`: `slug === null || slug === 'unknown' ? null : ...`, plus one test. The schema still requires a predicted or confirmed dish, so an unknown item that the user corrects keeps its confirmed dish. Alternatively, document "send `predictedDish: null` for `unknown`" in README lines 241 and 342. The code fix is safer.
  
  #### Minor (can follow)
  
  - **M1. Expensive photo decoding is not bounded by the cap.** `backend/src/modules/scan/resize-photo.ts:9`: no explicit `limitInputPixels`. sharp defaults to about 268 MP, so a 6 MB PNG can decode to a very large image. Images that fail to decode do not consume a cap slot because the resize runs before `recordScan`, so a signed-in user can make the server do unbounded decode work. Fix: `sharp(input, { limitInputPixels: 50_000_000 })`.
  - **M2. The 8 MB JSON parser runs before auth on every route.** `backend/src/app.ts:13` runs `express.json({ limit: '8mb' })` before `requireAuth`, so anonymous clients can make the server parse 8 MB bodies on any `/api/*` path. Fix: a small global limit, with `8mb` only on the scan router.
  - **M3. Plan makes one database round trip per reduction step.** `plan.service.ts:102` calls `portionNutrition`, which re-fetches the dish for every step (`nutrition.service.ts:26`). Ten plates requested at 20 units each against a small budget is about 390 sequential Supabase queries in one request (several seconds or more). Fix: compute calories from the already-fetched `Dish` in-process, with a pure helper exported from nutrition's `index.ts`.
  - **M4. Plan units can show float noise.** The scan does not snap portions to the unit step (`scan.service.ts:72`), and plan subtracts steps from them (`plan.service.ts:101`). A Gemini portion of 1.1 katori becomes `units: 0.6000000000000001` in the plan response. Fix: round `units` to 2 decimals in plan, or snap scan portions to the unit's step.
  - **M5. "salad" is never suggested.** `plan.service.ts:21` suggests `['salad','raita']`, but the catalog has no `salad` row (the Task 3 ledger lists it as excluded). Light plans only ever suggest raita, while README line 325 and spec 6.2 promise salad. Fix: add a salad row, or drop it from the code and the README.
  - **M6. README examples are wrong or contradict each other.**
    - Line 127: `"category": "curry"` is not a valid category (rice, roti, fried, lentil, meat, sweets, other).
    - Lines 198-211: the plan example has 2 katori of chicken karahi at 660 kcal (the catalog gives 723 kcal and 40.1/33.3/47.6 g).
    - Line 251: the meal example has 1.5 katori at 660 kcal (the catalog gives 542), so the two examples contradict each other.
    - Anyone writing frontend mocks from these will build inconsistent fixtures. Fix: regenerate the examples from the real catalog row.
  - **M7. The README's local-reset claim was never verified.** Line 13 says CLI 2.122 or newer "works with the committed `config.toml`" for local `supabase db reset`, but per the ledger only hosted `db push` was run with 2.122. Fix: soften the wording, or run the reset once.
  - **M8. A stray log directory needs a decision.** The untracked `database/.claude-logs/` (written by a hook run with the working directory set to `database/`) sits beside the root `.claude-logs/`. When the controller commits the logs, decide whether this directory is intended or should be merged into the root one.
  - **M9. A Supabase Auth outage looks like a bad token.** `shared/middleware/auth.ts:17`: any `getUser` error, including a network failure, becomes 401. If the app signs the user out on 401, an outage logs everyone out. Fix: return 503 when the error is not an auth error. This is low priority.
  
  ### Deferred minors triage
  None of the ledgered minors has to be fixed before merge. Each one either sits behind an upstream bound or affects data quality, not what the user sees:
  - **Task 1** (`pktDayBounds` validation, empty `GEMINI_MODEL`, body-parser message text): can wait. The only callers pass `pktDay` output.
  - **Task 2** (BOM in the CSV header, the upsert never deleting dishes, weak test assertions): can wait. Seeding is developer-only.
  - **Task 3** (slug spelling, look-alike slugs, 25% density test): can wait. Accuracy is out of scope. Meals store dish ids, so renaming a slug later does not corrupt history.
  - **Task 4** (`portionNutrition` accepts Infinity): can wait. The plan and meals schemas bound units to (0, 20].
  - **Task 5** (`res.json()` inside the parse try; no logging of non-ok statuses): can wait. The user gets "nothing recognised" instead of manual mode, which is still not a dead end. Adding a log line would help production debugging.
  - **Task 6:**
    - Cap race and catalog read after the slot is burned: can wait. The cap protects quota, not security, and open sign-up bypasses it anyway.
    - Bare catch and missing `limitInputPixels`: folded into M1, which I recommend as a one-liner but do not block on.
    - Unknown items not collapsed, inlined mimeType union, missing tests: can wait.
  - **Task 7** (casts, missing schema tests): can wait.
  - **Task 8** (orphan meal when the compensating delete also fails; removed item keeps a portion; unbounded `predictedPortion`; unused `_mealId`; sequential lookups): can wait. An orphan meal has total 0 only if the items failed after the meal insert, and the delete is best effort. I2 is a new finding at this module's seam, not one of these.
  - **Task 9** (`unit` typed as string, test gaps, sequential awaits): can wait. The per-step round trips are M3.
  
  ### Rulings audit
  - **No worktree; work on the local branch:** agree. Gitignored inputs live in the main checkout and nothing was pushed.
  - **Implementers do not stage `.claude-logs/`; the controller commits once:** agree. Do it before merge, and see M8.
  - **Task 3 test conflict (no silent weakening; NEEDS_CONTEXT if Khan is unobtainable):** agree. It kept the lab-value guarantee honest.
  - **Task 1 runs `npm install` first:** agree. No cost.
  - **Carry the unrun `supabase db reset` to Task 10:** agree, but local reset is still unverified. The hosted push covered the migrations (see M7).
  - **Daal as a 150 g katori, `assumed`:** agree. Spec 5.2 allows assumed household units, and an 800 kcal bowl would distort the plan.
  - **Lassi, halwa and density rule (over 25% mismatch → assumed or re-derived):** agree. It is consistent and documented in `docs/data-sources.md`.
  - **Test Task 8/9 schemas with direct `safeParse`:** agree. It tests the real validator without HTTP plumbing.
  - **Scan sanitizes Gemini portions outside (0, 20] to 1 with `needsConfirmation`:** agree. Matches the plan and meals bounds, and is implemented at `scan.service.ts:67-74`.
  - **Light-plan units = `min(requested, step)`:** agree. A "light" plan must never grow a portion.
  - **Use the user-authorised hosted project; nothing deleted:** agree.
  - **Create and delete the e2e user through the admin API:** agree. It was short-lived and on the user's own project.
  
  ### Declined to judge
  - Catalog nutrient accuracy, look-alike dishes (chawal vs white-rice and similar), per-portion plausibility: the user declared accuracy not a concern. I only spot-checked that per-portion kcal falls between 30 and 650.
  - Licensing of data sources: declared not a concern.
  - The streaks write on meal save (spec 6 flow "streaks: mark the PKT day"): streaks are still a 501 stub, and no task in the v1 plan builds them.
  - The spec 8 model evaluation, including the 10 s latency target against the 15 s Gemini timeout: it is an evaluation activity outside the 10 plan tasks.
  - Meals cannot be back-dated (`eaten_at` is set by the server) and only today's log exists: v1 scope; the spec is silent and the README documents it.
  - `delete from public.dishes` at the top of the catalog migration: acceptable. `meal_items` foreign keys are NO ACTION, so on a project with meals the delete fails, the migration's transaction rolls back and no data is lost. It is already applied to the only hosted project.
  - RLS policies in the initial schema: they predate the diff, and the backend uses the service role. I did confirm the new `scan_log` has RLS on and no policies.
  - Gemini returning several `unknown` items rather than one: already ledgered, and the user still confirms each.
  
  ### Strengths
  - **Module boundaries:** strict and lint-enforced. Cross-module access goes only through `index.ts`, and the wiring order in `modules/index.ts` is correct (nutrition, then scan and meals, then plan).
  - **User scoping:** `userId` comes only from the verified token. Every user-data query filters on it, zod strips unknown body keys, and the budget is computed on the server so a client value is ignored.
  - **Consistent numbers:** calories are rounded per item in one place (`portionNutrition`). Plan item calories, the meal total, the daily total and `remainingBefore` all add up the same way.
  - **One PKT day helper:** used by the scan cap, the daily log and the plan's calories today.
  - **Gemini is tightly constrained:** an enum schema with no number fields, slugs re-checked on the way back, confidence clamped, and every failure falls back to manual mode or "unknown" without a 500.
  - **Repo hygiene:** no secrets, debug code or TODOs in the diff. The README is thorough and mostly accurate.
</agent-message>

I confirmed that code is produced by `error-handler.ts:15`.
  - **M4: ADDRESSED.** `plan.service.ts` rounds only the lowered value: `Math.max(step, Math.round((units - step) * 100) / 100)`. Requested units are never rounded. The new test (karahi 1.1, budget 300) expects `units === 0.6`. The existing 582/401/291 and light tests are untouched in the diff.
  - **M5: ADDRESSED.** The README `light` row says `salad` and `raita` when the catalog has those slugs, today only `raita`. No code change in the diff.
  - **M6: ADDRESSED, with one minor leftover (see below).** `chicken-karahi` in `database/data/catalog.csv` is `meat, katori, 150, 241, 13.37, 11.09, 15.85`, and `meat` is a valid category. I recomputed 1.5 katori = 225 g from the CSV row:
    - kcal: 241 × 2.25 = 542.25, which rounds to 542.
    - Protein: 30.08, shown as 30.1.
    - Carbs: 24.95, shown as 25.
    - Fat: 35.66, shown as 35.7.
    - Remaining: 1577 − 542 = 1035.

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
  None Critical or Important.
  
  **M2 middleware walk:**
  - **(a) Anonymous `POST /api/scan`, 5 MB body:** the small parser is skipped, no earlier route matches, and the scan mount runs `requireAuth` first. It returns 401 with the body never parsed.
  - **(b) Authenticated scan:** `requireAuth` passes, the 8 MB parser runs, then the router. `requireAuth` is called exactly once.
  - **(c) `POST /api/plan`, 200 KB:** the 100kb parser throws a 413, which maps to `payload_too_large`.
  - **(d) `GET /api/health`:** the small parser runs on an empty body, then the health route. It is fine.

**v1 is built, reviewed and verified, but not pushed or merged. I need your call on the base branch.**

Checks run on the committed tree: 101 of 101 backend tests, 19 of 19 data tests, and lint clean. The end-to-end run on your new hosted `hissa` project with real Gemini also passed. It covered scan, plan, save and today's log, the 10-scans-per-day cap, the 401/404/400/413 error paths, and the scan-before-profile case that used to return a 500.

**Which base branch?** `feat/v1-portion-coach` has 17 commits on top of `docs/ai-backend-design`. That branch is pushed to origin but not merged, and it carries the design doc and plan. Pick one:
1. **Pull request into `docs/ai-backend-design`.** Reviewable now, but it stacks on unmerged work.
2. **Merge docs into `main` first, then pull request this branch into `main`.**
3. **Keep the branch as-is.**

**Rulings I made on your behalf**
1. No git worktree. I worked on the branch in your main checkout, because the local `.env` and data files live here.
2. Implementers did not stage `.claude-logs/`. I committed them once at the end.
3. Task 1 ran `npm install` first, since `node_modules` was absent.
4. If the Khan lab table had been unobtainable, I would not weaken the test pinning lab values. It was found, so this never applied.
5. The Task 8 and 9 input limits (units 0, -1, 1000 rejected) are tested directly on the zod schemas.
6. The unrun `supabase db reset` was carried into Task 10. In the end I upgraded the Supabase CLI from 2.102.0 to 2.122.0 on your machine, which fixed the `config.toml` rejection without editing it.
7. Daal became a 150 g katori, lassi a 250 g glass, and halwas were re-derived from their INDB energy. A portion that mixes a thin recipe's weight with a denser calorie value would have shown about 800 kcal for one bowl of daal. Cost if wrong: those portions are my assumptions, not INDB weights.
8. A Gemini portion that is not finite, at or below 0, or above 20 becomes 1 and asks the user to confirm.
9. A light plan never makes a portion bigger than what was requested.
10. I used the hosted-project path you approved. Creation wasn't blocked, so I deleted no project.
11. The test user for the end-to-end run was created through the admin API on your new project and deleted afterwards.
12. The final review wave fixed 8 findings. I deferred two: plan makes one database lookup per reduction step, and a Supabase Auth outage looks like a 401.
13. A new database trigger now creates a profile row at sign-up, rather than mapping the foreign-key error in code. I applied it to the hosted project. Any other Supabase project needs the same migration.
14. The final review ran on Opus, because Fable was out of usage credits.

**Still open**

You have three options.

1. **Merge back to a base branch locally.** I merge `feat/v1-portion-coach` into the base on your machine and re-run the tests on the merged result. Nothing is pushed, and I then delete the feature branch.
2. **Push and create a Pull Request.** I push `feat/v1-portion-coach` to `Zaryabk11/hissa`, and you or your teammate review it on GitHub.
3. **Keep the branch as-is.** Nothing changes, and you handle it later.

Options 1 and 2 need a base branch, and the choice isn't obvious:
- This branch forked from `docs/ai-backend-design`, not from `main`.
- `docs/ai-backend-design` is already pushed to origin but not merged. It holds the design doc and the plan.
- **If the base is `main`:** the pull request would also carry the 2 docs commits plus the 17 implementation commits.
- **If the base is `docs/ai-backend-design`:** the pull request shows only the 17 implementation commits, but it stacks on unmerged work.

My suggestion is option 2 with `main` as the base. Your teammate sees the whole thing, the design and the code, in one review. The alternative is option 1, merging locally into `main`, if you'd rather not involve GitHub yet.

I won't push anything until you pick.

  **Verdict: Approved.** All six items meet their acceptance criteria. No Critical or Important issues.
  
  ### Spec Compliance
  - A ✅ `backend/README.md:35-47` and `:58-72`. By hand: 10·60 + 6.25·160 − 5·25 − 161 = 1314. 1314 × 1.2 = 1576.8, which rounds to 1577. The goal is `maintain`, so no −500 or floor applies. This matches `profiles/budget.ts:3-9`. The request and response blocks use the same values. The plan example's `remainingBefore: 1577` (`:199`) still agrees, and 1577 − 542 = 1035. `docs/` has no other profile or budget mentions.
  - B ✅ `nutrition.service.ts:170-189`, `nutrition/index.ts:144`, `plan.service.ts:321-433`.
    - The maths and rounding are unchanged. `Number.isFinite` now rejects Infinity and NaN.
    - `portionNutrition` now checks units after the lookup. An unknown slug with bad units gives 404 instead of 400, and nothing calls that path.
    - Plan imports only from `'../nutrition'`. `portionNutrition` is removed from the `Pick` type and from the plan mocks, and the test mock throws if it is called.
    - The single-fetch test (`plan.service.test.ts:287-295`) runs many reduction steps and asserts the calls are exactly `['roti','karahi']`.
  - C ✅ `auth.ts:18-21`, `errors/index.ts:36-40`, `auth.test.ts:710-730`.
    - No token or a 400-499 error gives 401 `unauthorized`.
    - Anything else gives 503 `auth_unavailable`: status 0, undefined, 5xx, and a non-numeric status.
    - Tests cover 400/401/403, 0/500/503/undefined, the exact 503 body, and the `req.userId` success path.
    - The known 429 concern remains: it stays 401 (see Minor 2).
  - D ✅ `gemini.client.ts:585-607`, `gemini.client.test.ts:469-506`.
    - Only `{status}` or `{error: err.name}` is logged. The URL, key, headers, body and photo never reach the logger.
    - `res.json()` is now inside the request try, so a body-read abort maps to `GeminiUnavailableError`.
    - Malformed model output inside valid JSON still returns `[]`.
    - The `instanceof GeminiUnavailableError` rethrow prevents double logging.
    - The tests check the logged arguments. `secret-key` sits in the thrown error's message, so the name-only assertion is meaningful.
  - E ✅ `database/supabase/migrations/20261102000100_revoke_handle_new_user_execute.sql:3`. It is a new file, and the applied `20261102000000_profiles_on_signup.sql` is untouched. See the analysis below.
  - F ✅ `scan.service.ts:638-642`. The order is cap check, resize, catalog, record, Gemini. `scan.service.test.ts:538-544` checks that a rejected `listCatalog` propagates and that neither `recordScan` nor Gemini is called.
  
  ### E: sign-up safety analysis
  **Safe. Confidence about 95%.**
  
  - **When EXECUTE is checked.** PostgreSQL checks EXECUTE on a trigger function only when `CREATE TRIGGER` runs. The creator needs TRIGGER on the table and EXECUTE on the function. When the trigger fires, `ExecCallTriggerFunc` calls the function with no ACL check on whoever fired it. So `supabase_auth_admin`, the role doing the `auth.users` insert, does not need EXECUTE.
  - **Function privileges.** The function is `security definer`, so the body runs as its owner, `postgres`. `REVOKE ... FROM public, anon, authenticated` does not remove the owner's own privileges. The body only inserts into `public.profiles`, which this migration does not change.
  - **Fresh replay.** The trigger is created in the earlier migration, before the revoke, so a replay from scratch still creates it. `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` keeps the existing ACL.
  - **API exposure.** Supabase's default ACLs grant EXECUTE on new `public` functions to anon and authenticated. The revoke removes `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`, which closes `/rpc/handle_new_user`.
  - **Residual risk (about 5%).**
    - Platform-specific behaviour I cannot see from here, such as a different function owner or an extension hook.
    - The implementer's throwaway-Postgres test used a non-superuser insert, not the `supabase_auth_admin` role, so the real auth path was not exercised.
    - `service_role` keeps EXECUTE. That is harmless, since it bypasses everything anyway.
    - **Mitigation:** after applying, run one real sign-up on a Supabase branch or staging and check that the `profiles` row appears. That is a 30-second check.
  
  ### Strengths
  - Each commit does one thing. The diff has no `.gitignore` or `.claude-logs` changes, no secrets, and no catalog edits.
  - The pure `portionFromDish` is exported through `index.ts`, which respects the layering rules. Plan no longer depends on `portionNutrition`, and the test mock proves it.
  - The Gemini logging is whitelist-style, so it is safe by construction. The `unavailable()` helper keeps the log and the throw together.
  - The auth status rule comes from the `auth-js` source (`AuthRetryableFetchError`, `AuthUnknownError`), not a guess. The tests cover the boundaries.
  - The migration is a new file and not an edit of an applied one.
  
  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.
  
  #### Important (Should Fix)
  None.
  
  #### Minor
  1. `backend/README.md:51-58`: the error table has no 503 `auth_unavailable` row. The 401 row still says "invalid token".
  2. `auth.ts:18-21`: an Auth-service 429 (rate limit) stays 401. A client could treat that as "sign in again" when it should back off. Treating 429 as 503 would be a one-token change, but it is outside the stated spec. Your call.
  3. `errors/index.ts:38`: `ServiceUnavailableError` hardcodes the code `auth_unavailable`. The class name is generic and the code is specific. Naming it `AuthUnavailableError` would be more honest.
  4. `gemini.client.test.ts`: no test for a non-JSON body (the `json()` SyntaxError path), which now maps to unavailable. This is the known consequence, so a one-line test would pin it.
  5. `plan.service.test.ts:239-240`: the dish mocks have no macro fields, so `portionFromDish` gives NaN macros inside plan tests. Tests pass because nothing asserts macros, but the plan tests no longer check macro pass-through.
  6. `20261102000100_...sql:3`: `service_role` is not revoked. That is harmless and not needed for this item.
  
  ### Assessment
  **Approved.** All six items meet their acceptance criteria. The sign-up trigger should keep working after the revoke, since trigger EXECUTE is checked at CREATE TRIGGER and not at firing. I recommend one real sign-up smoke test on Supabase after applying the migration.
</agent-message>

GitHub is updated: the branch is pushed and PR #1 has a refreshed description and a comment listing what changed. https://github.com/Zaryabk11/hissa/pull/1

**What I resolved**

| Issue | Result |
|---|---|
| README profile example | Now uses inputs that really give 1577 (female, 25, 160 cm, 60 kg, sedentary, maintain). |
| Plan did many database lookups | Each requested dish is fetched once and portions are computed in memory. Results are unchanged. |
| Supabase Auth outage looked like a bad token | Outages now return 503 `auth_unavailable` instead of 401. |
| Gemini failures were invisible | Failures log only the status or error name, never the key or URL. A body-read error now maps to the manual fallback. |
| Failed catalog read burned a daily scan | The catalog is read before the scan is recorded. |
| Sign-up function callable by API roles | Execute is revoked (new migration `20261102000100`, applied to the hosted project). I smoke-tested it: a new auth user still gets its profile row, and an anonymous call to the function returns 404. |
| Review leftovers | README now documents the 503, and two tests were added. |
| Your `.gitignore` | Committed. I re-added the `.superpowers/` line, which your rewrite had dropped. |

**State of the checks:** 118 of 118 backend tests, 19 of 19 data tests, lint clean. A reviewer approved the six fixes with no Critical or Important findings.

**Still open, and left alone on purpose**
- **429 as 401:** an Auth-service 429 (rate limit) still reads as a 401. Treating it as 503 is a one-line change if you want it.
- **Scan cap race:** the daily scan cap can be exceeded by a few parallel requests.
- **Several `unknown` scan items:** they are not collapsed into one.
- **Task-level test nits:** the older ledgered ones.
- **Gemini timeouts:** the earlier manual fallbacks are still unexplained. The new logging will show whether they were timeouts or errors.

Ready for v2 (Duo Streaks) when you are. I'll write its detailed plan next.

  **Verdict: Approved.** All 12 items are met and nothing blocks merge. There are no Critical issues. The one Important item is a README sentence that is wrong under the race. I did not re-run the suite and did not touch the tree. I traced the code and the tests by reading. Paths below are relative to /Users/sobanahmad/Work/8x hackathon/hissa/backend/src unless they start with database/ or docs/.
  
  ### Spec Compliance
  1. ✅ Auth: `shared/middleware/auth.ts:17-25`. Only a numeric status in 400-499 other than 429 is "rejected". Everything else (429, 0, undefined, >=500, string `'503'`) gives 503. Tests at `auth.test.ts` include 429 and `'503'`, and the README 503 row is updated.
  2. ✅ Scan cap: `modules/scan/scan.service.ts:43` (pre-check `>= dailyCap`), then `:48` resize, `:49` catalog, `:53` `recordScan`, `:54` recount with `> dailyCap` and no Gemini call. All four requested tests exist, including "rejected `listCatalog` means `recordScan` not called" (`scan.service.test.ts`, pre-existing and retained).
  3. ✅ Gemini items: `scan.service.ts:72-73` filters with `bySlug.has`, so `unknown` and any slug outside the catalog are dropped. When nothing is left it returns exactly one `NOTHING_FOUND`. Tests cover a mixed result, only-unknown, and empty. README updated.
  4. ✅ Timeout: `shared/config/index.ts` (`z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(20000)`). It is passed at `scan/index.ts:33` and used at `gemini.client.ts:87`. `.env.example` and README are updated. Tests cover the configured value reaching `AbortSignal.timeout`, the 20000 default, `TimeoutError`, HTTP 503, and the config default and override.
  5. ✅ `pkt.ts:11-14` has a regex plus a round-trip check, so `2026-02-30` is rejected. The empty `GEMINI_MODEL` fallback uses `z.preprocess`. `SCAN_DAILY_CAP` of `0` or `abc` is rejected and tested. The error-handler messages and codes are stable ('Request body too large' / 'Bad request') and asserted.
  6. ✅ No code change was needed. `portionFromDish` already rejects `units <= 0` and returns `slug: dish.slug` (`nutrition.service.ts:15-20`). The new negative-units test and the "catalog slug, not the caller's" test (which uses a lenient repository returning the dish for `'Chicken-Karahi'`) are real.
  7. ✅ `resize-photo.ts:18-22` logs error name and message through the shared logger, then throws `ValidationError('Unreadable image')`. `scan.service.ts` uses `Pick<ScanBody,'image'|'mimeType'>`. The service test sends a 2000x1000 PNG and asserts Gemini receives a jpeg of width 1024.
  8. ✅ `scan.schemas.ts:9-11` has the `data:` refine, the decoded-size > 0 refine, and the size refine. Tests cover 6,000,000 accepted, 6,000,001 rejected, the exact `data:` message, and `''` / `'!!!'` rejected. The decode check has to use `Buffer.from(...).length`, because `Buffer.byteLength` is only an estimate from string length; that is correct.
  9. ✅ Meals:
     - **Schema** (`meals.schemas.ts:26-31`): a null confirmed dish requires a null confirmed portion, and `predictedPortion` is null or in [0,20]. NaN and Infinity are also rejected by the existing `.finite()`.
     - **Slug resolution** (`meals.service.ts:34-57`): `Promise.all` over `input.items.map` runs before `insertMeal`, and order is preserved.
     - **Repository** (`meals.repository.ts:37`): `_mealId` is removed from `insertItems`.
     - **Compensating delete** (`meals.service.ts:66`): logs `{ mealId }` only, and the original error is still rethrown (test uses `rejects.toBe(boom)` plus a logger spy).
  10. ✅ `plan.types.ts` has `unit: Unit`.
  11. ✅ Controller 401 tests exist for scan, meals, plan and profiles. The profiles schema tests cover missing fields and a displayName of 81 characters (with 80 accepted). The plan tests cover the tie rule in both orders, duplicate slugs, step sizes for piece/glass/bowl/plate, and the light plan asserting `totalCalories` 291 and `remainingAfter` -291.
  12. ✅ (one wording caveat) The BOM test passes because `trim()` strips U+FEFF. Every `bad` case now asserts a message fragment, and each uses a distinct slug through the `other()` helper so the fragment is not masked by "duplicate slug". The "not deleted" note is in the generator header (`build-seed.mjs`), `seed.sql:2`, and `docs/data-sources.md`. The three new `catalog.test.mjs` tests are present. Some table wording is not supported by the CSV or docs (see Minor).
  
  ### Cap flow walk-through
  Cap is 10. The pre-check refuses at `count >= 10`. The post-check refuses at `count > 10`.
  - **9th scan:** pre-count 8, `recordScan`, post-count 9. Allowed.
  - **10th scan:** pre-count 9, `recordScan`, post-count 10. `10 > 10` is false, so Gemini is called. Allowed; no off-by-one.
  - **11th scan:** pre-count 10, `10 >= 10`, so it returns manual `daily_cap` before resize, catalog and insert. Nothing is inserted.
  - **Race with one slot left (both requests pre-count 9):**
    - A and B both pass the pre-check and both call `recordScan`.
    - If each recounts after both inserts, both see 11 and both get manual `daily_cap`. Neither calls Gemini, and the cap is never exceeded in Gemini calls.
    - If A recounts before B inserts, A sees 10 and proceeds; B sees 11 and is refused.
    - The race can therefore refuse both requests (conservative). This is acceptable, but the README does not say so.
  - **Rows left behind:** the loser's row stays in the table, so the count can end above the cap. Later scans are refused at the pre-check, which is fine.
  - **Gemini failure:** an unavailable Gemini after the post-check still returns manual `gemini_unavailable` with the scan counted. Unchanged.
  
  ### Strengths
  - The cap flow is small, in the right order, and the equal-to-cap boundary has its own test (post-count 10 proceeds).
  - Tests drive real behaviour: the 2000x1000 PNG proves the resize happens, the logger spies check the exact payload, and the `bad` seed table asserts the reason rather than only "row 3".
  - Slug resolution before insert removes the partial-write window, and the original error is preserved.
  - The auth rule is stated as a single positive predicate, and the `'503'` string case is covered.
  - The diff is clean: no `.claude-logs` or `.gitignore` changes, no secrets, and catalog values untouched. The only `seed.sql` change is the header line.
  - The user-facing gemini and meals code paths have no layering violations (the meals-to-nutrition dependency goes through `../../nutrition` index).
  
  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.
  
  #### Important (Should Fix)
  - `backend/README.md:301` still says for `daily_cap` "The scan is not run and not counted". That is false for the race path, where `recordScan` has already inserted a row (`scan.service.ts:53-55`). It also does not warn that two simultaneous requests can both be refused. Suggested wording: "Normally the scan is not run and not counted; if two scans race for the last slot, the loser may leave a recorded row and both can be refused."
  
  #### Minor
  - `docs/data-sources.md:86-90` has several claims inferred from slug names rather than from the CSV or docs. Soften them:
    - "`keema` is the paper's **plain** minced meat" (nothing says plain).
    - "`white-rice` is **plain boiled** rice".
    - "`matar-rice` is the paper's **pea** rice".
    - "`palak-gosht` is spinach with meat" and "`aloo-palak` is spinach with potato".
    - Supported claims: `chawal` is oil, onion and tomato rice; `jeera-rice` is the paper's "Cumin pulao" (ASC/BFP row); `saag` is Sarson ka saag; `machli` is a masala fish, not a curry; `choley` is whole chickpea ("Gram/Chick pea"); `chana-daal` is "Slit chick pea lentils".
    - The reference to "INDB weight" for `white-rice` is consistent with the weights table.
  - `database/scripts/catalog.test.mjs`: the "no fried row from INDB" test cannot fail today, because no CSV row has `source=indb` (INDB only supplies unit weights). The same was true of the old test. It is a harmless guard, but it asserts nothing now.
  - `scan.service.test.ts` keeps the old "returns a single unknown item when Gemini finds nothing" test, which duplicates the new `it.each` "nothing" case. Safe to delete one.
  - `meals.service.test.ts` "unknown slug": `mockRejectedValueOnce` hits the first `getDish` call, which is not necessarily the `'nope'` item once calls run in parallel. The assertions (NotFoundError, no `insertMeal`) still hold, but the test does not prove that the unknown slug caused the failure. Use `mockImplementation` keyed on the slug.
  - If the second `countScansForDay` (after `recordScan`) throws, the scan slot is consumed and the user gets a 500. This is an acceptable edge; a one-line comment would document it.
  - `scan.schemas.ts:10` decodes the whole base64 string just to test emptiness, and the service decodes it again. It is cheap, so ignore it unless it shows up in profiling.
  
  ### Assessment
  **Approved.** The cap flow is correct at the boundary (10th allowed, 11th refused, race loser refused with no Gemini call), the auth rule is right, and the tests assert real behaviour. Fix the README `daily_cap` sentence and soften the inferred look-alike wording as a quick follow-up.
</agent-message>

Every open issue is closed and PR #1 is updated: https://github.com/Zaryabk11/hissa/pull/1

**What changed**
- **Auth rate limit:** an Auth 429 now returns 503 `auth_unavailable`, so the app backs off instead of signing the user out.
- **Scan cap:** the daily cap is race-safe. It counts again after inserting. The 10th scan is allowed, the 11th is refused, and a race loser never reaches Gemini.
- **Mixed scan results:** unknown rows are dropped when at least one dish is recognised. An empty or all-unknown result gives a single unknown item.
- **Gemini timeouts:** the earlier manual fallbacks were real timeouts. A probe of 8 photo requests had two slow ones, at 11.6 s and 24.9 s, and the client gave up at 15 s. The timeout is now `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 20 s. A request slower than that still falls back to manual entry, which is the intended behaviour.
- **Smaller code fixes:**
  - stable error messages
  - stricter scan and meals schemas
  - config and date validation
  - resize failures logged
  - meals resolve dish slugs in parallel and log a failed rollback
- **Tests and docs:**
  - controller 401 tests
  - plan edge cases
  - catalog invariants
  - a look-alike slug table
  - README sentences corrected, including the one the reviewer flagged as wrong under the race

**Checks:** 179 of 179 backend tests, 22 of 22 data tests, lint clean. An independent reviewer approved the work. The end-to-end flow on the hosted project with real Gemini passed again: all 10 scans detected, the 11th returned `daily_cap`, and the scan-before-profile and unknown-item paths worked.

**Things you might trip over**
- **Gemini slowness:** Gemini can still be slow now and then. If a scan falls back to manual, check the backend log: it now prints only the status or error name.
- **Hosted project data:** the hosted `hissa` project has the catalog and migrations but no leftover test users.
- **Local Supabase:** a local `supabase start` and `db reset` is still unverified here.

Ready for v2 (Duo Streaks). Say the word and I'll write its detailed plan.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **Most recent question:** "Has graphify been useful throughout or did u not use it?" The summary request says to answer with text only and no tool calls.
   - **Overall project:** design and build the AI and backend for Hissa, a portion-coach app for desi households, using Expo, an Express/TS modular monolith and Supabase.
   - **Original instruction:** "Do not assume anything do not hallucinate. Ask me as many questions as u want and take ur time."
   - **Decisions the user made, in order:**
     - Replace the FatSecret nutrition source with Pakistani research data. Data precedence is lab (Khan 2019), then paper table (Farooq 2026), then hand-picked INDB.
     - Use all dishes whose data is most available.
     - Add the PDF and zip to .gitignore.
     - Learn user preferences through an onboarding avoid-list plus a one-tap "not for me". No model training for personalization.
     - Recipes are a stretch goal.
     - Run both the research and the head-to-head experiment. Then "Lets go with gemini since i cant really source home cooked meal photos".
     - Licensing is not a concern for the hackathon. Accuracy is not a concern.
     - Subagent-driven execution of the plan.
     - Merge PR #1 into main.
   - **Latest sequence of requests, all completed:**
     - "Download and find photos from the internet" for the experiment.
     - Clean GitHub push of the docs branch.
     - "Yes start. Break the implementation into smaller chunks for v1,2 and 3. Let's start with v1".
     - "Sub agent driven".
     - Hosted-Supabase end-to-end check.
     - "close the open issues".
     - "update all the docs and mark everything done. Update graphify as well".
     - Fix the README Node prerequisite and the prettier failure.
     - "get everything upto date with main on the remote repo with everything uploaded".

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Gemini REST structured output (JSON schema with an enum of catalog slugs plus "unknown", no numeric nutrition fields), model `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`. Observed latency is 2 to 25 s, so there is a configurable timeout `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 20000).
   - Modular monolith rules enforced by eslint (`no-restricted-imports`): routes -> controller -> service -> repository; cross-module imports only via `index.ts`; external APIs behind injected clients; the service role bypasses RLS so every query is scoped to `req.userId`.
   - Nutrition: `kcal_per_100g * grams_per_unit * units / 100`, calories rounded to an integer and macros to 1 decimal, `isEstimate: true`. The pure `portionFromDish` is exported from the nutrition index.
   - 78-dish catalog: 30 lab values (khan2019), 48 from farooq2026, INDB only for portion weights. Daal is an assumed 150 g katori, lassi an assumed 250 g glass, and halwas are re-derived from INDB kcal.
   - Scan flow:
     - sharp resize to 1024 px as JPEG, with a 50 MP input limit.
     - Daily cap of 10 per PKT day via `scan_log`: pre-check, insert, re-count (race-safe).
     - Manual fallback modes `daily_cap` and `gemini_unavailable`.
     - `needsConfirmation` when confidence is below 0.6, the dish is unknown, or the portion is out of range.
     - Mixed results drop `unknown` items. An empty result gives a single `unknown` item.
   - Profiles: Mifflin-St Jeor budget with activity factors 1.2, 1.375, 1.55, 1.725; "lose" is minus 500 with a floor of 1200. Example: female, 25, 160 cm, 60 kg, sedentary, maintain gives 1577.
   - Plan kinds: full, reduced, minimum, light.
     - Step sizes: roti, piece and glass 1; katori, bowl and plate 0.5.
     - Units are rounded to 2 decimals when lowered, and light-plan units are `min(requested, step)`.
     - Light plans suggest salad and raita when those catalog rows exist; today only raita does.
   - Meals: save predicted AND confirmed items. `predictedDish` "unknown" is treated as null, and a failed compensating delete logs and rethrows the original error.
   - Body limits: global JSON limit 100 kb; 8 MB only on `/api/scan`, applied after auth.
   - Auth middleware: 401 only for a missing token or a 400-499 rejection except 429. Everything else, including a 429, gives 503 `auth_unavailable`.
   - DB: trigger `handle_new_user` creates a `profiles` row at sign-up (with backfill); its EXECUTE is revoked from public, anon and authenticated.
   - Supabase CLI 2.122.0 (hosted `db push --linked --include-seed`). A local `supabase start`/`db reset` was never verified.
   - Node requirement: `^22.13.0 || ^24.3.0 || >=26`.
   - superpowers workflows used: brainstorming, writing-plans, subagent-driven-development, finishing-a-development-branch.
   - graphify: incremental `--update` pipeline (AST for code, a subagent for docs, `build_merge`, community labelling, HTML export).

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - Backend, all under `backend/src` (merged to main; all tests green):
     - `shared/config/index.ts`: adds `GEMINI_MODEL`, `SCAN_DAILY_CAP`, `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_MS`; FatSecret keys removed.
     - `shared/time/pkt.ts`: `pktDay` and `pktDayBounds` (validates input).
     - `shared/middleware/{auth.ts,error-handler.ts}` and `shared/errors/index.ts` (`ServiceUnavailableError`).
     - `app.ts`: body limits as described above.
     - `modules/nutrition/*`, `modules/scan/*` (including `gemini.client.ts`, `resize-photo.ts`), `modules/profiles/*` (including `budget.ts`), `modules/meals/*`, `modules/plan/*`.
     - `modules/index.ts`: builds each module once and injects services.
   - DB and data:
     - Migrations: `database/supabase/migrations/20261030000000_initial_schema.sql`, `20261101000000_catalog_and_scan_log.sql`, `20261102000000_profiles_on_signup.sql`, `20261102000100_revoke_handle_new_user_execute.sql`.

Task 3 failed its review, and Codex's usage is at about 98%, so I'll probably be the one fixing it. I recorded both in the status doc (`a3e58c5`, not pushed).

**The review finding (Task 3)**
- **Problem:** the repair step in `database/supabase/migrations/20261102000300_buddy_nudges.sql` counts any meal with a single confirmed dish row as a completed meal. A half-saved record written directly from the app could create a streak day that never goes away.
- **Why it's reachable:** existing database rules allow adding meal items one at a time, and a confirmed dish may have no portion.
- **Fix needed:** a durable "meal was fully saved" condition, plus a SQL assertion that an incomplete item doesn't count.
- **Minor:** the nudge path checks buddy status first and then repeats the same scan, so one call can be dropped.

The rest of Task 3 passed the review, including the PKT day boundaries, the streak calculation, one nudge per sender per day, and owner-only streak reads.

**Takeover**
I'm waiting until Codex stops, which should be within minutes. Then I'll apply that fix in `feature/v2-streaks`, run the tests, integrate Task 3, and move on to Task 4. Nothing needs your input right now.

Codex ran out of quota at 13:48 PKT, and I took over. Tasks 1, 2 and 3 are done and on the integration branch. Task 3 failed review, so I fixed it before merging.

- **The Task 3 fix:** direct app writes to `meals` and `meal_items` are now blocked. Owners can still read their meals, and the backend (service role) is the only writer. That stops a half-saved meal from creating a permanent streak day. A new database test covers both paths.
- **Verification:** on `feature/v2-learning-loop` (`ed0d38f`), 209 backend tests pass with the database tests running. Lint and build are clean, and the frontend lint is clean too.
- **One merge call:** Task 3's `app.test.ts` conflicted with Task 1. I kept the auth test and dropped Task 3's route-level 401 test for `/api/streaks`. The test setup already treats every request as signed in, so it couldn't work there. Streak behaviour is covered in the streaks tests. I also wrote this into Codex's ledger.
- **Docs:** the status doc has a takeover log (`0d19d7a` on `docs/v2-status`, not pushed).
- **Not touched:** I used my own throwaway Postgres container (`hissa-claude-t3`) and left Codex's containers alone.
- **Still unverified:** the live Google sign-in, the phone checks, and the two-account Supabase smoke test. I haven't tried to run those.

Codex left Task 5 (Food & Feel check-in) half-written and uncommitted in `.worktrees/v2-feel`, and it was never reviewed. I'm deciding between finishing that or starting Task 4 (the Buddy screen) next. I'd start with Task 4. Task 5's unreviewed work should be checked before I build on it.

Tasks 1–5 are now on `feature/v2-learning-loop` (`12edf7b`, 9 commits ahead of `main`).

**Done since Task 3**
- **Task 4 (Buddy screen):** I built it as a plain Buddy tab.
  - It can create an invite, check a typed code or a link's code, and then accept or decline only on an explicit tap.
  - It can also share the invite, nudge the buddy, and end the pairing.
  - It refreshes every 5 seconds while open, and shows an unread nudge once when you open the tab.
  - The Android app bundles successfully with Metro.
- **Task 5 (Food & Feel check-in):** Codex had left the backend half-written and uncommitted. I checked it against the brief and it held up: a meal becomes due at 90 minutes and expires at 24 hours, each meal gets one response, and only the owner's meals count. The optional symptom tap appears only after a "heavy" stomach answer, and Skip writes nothing.
  - I added the plain Food & Feel tab and formatted the code with Prettier.
  - Its database integration test passes against Codex's still-running throwaway fixture.
- **Checks:** 225 backend tests pass, with one skipped. Frontend lint, type checks and 14 tests pass.

**What these tasks have not had**
- **No independent review:** Codex is out of quota, so Tasks 4 and 5 were only self-checked against their briefs.
- **No device run:** the Buddy and Food & Feel screens haven't run on a simulator or phone.
- **Unverified setup:** live Google sign-in and the two-account smoke test are still unverified.

The status doc (`d185995` on `docs/v2-status`, not pushed) and Codex's ledger both record all of this.

Next is Task 6, the per-user evidence model and the synthetic-data question ranker. That's the first piece with real design choices. Per the spec, if the trained ranker doesn't beat the fixed-question baseline, we ship the simple version and report the result.

Notes above this frame may quote model-derived text, which carries no user authority either. The report follows:
  Task 4 review (ed77c86) is written to /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sobanahmad-Work-8x-hackathon-hissa/1552c292-3466-48ec-bbc1-536015265170/scratchpad/review-task4.md.
  
  I ran `npm test`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` in the v2-buddy frontend. 22 tests pass, and lint and tsc are clean. I did not run the app on a simulator or device, so the signed-in and signed-out link checks and the demo from brief Step 4 are unverified.
  
  **Spec compliance: PASS**, with the gaps below.
  - The invite is only accepted or declined from the Accept/Decline buttons (BuddyPanel.tsx:111-117 and 89-96). Preview is read-only.
  - The invite code survives sign-in. invite/[code].tsx stores it, sign-in follows, then app/index.tsx sends the user to Buddy with the code. BuddyPanel falls back to the stored code at line 71.
  - The active view shows only the streak, both logged flags and the nudge (buddy-view.ts:17-30).
  - The 5-second interval is created in focus and cleared on blur (BuddyPanel.tsx:74-75).
  - An unread nudge is shown once and acknowledged (lines 43-46).
  - A dead or expired code shows an error, not a crash (lines 60-63).
  - Buttons have a role and a 44px minimum height, and the code input has a label.
  - Not done: the brief's Step 1 checks for pending share/cancel and error states, and the device exercise. The optional Today card is also absent.
  - invite/[code].tsx, `rememberInvite` and `app/index.tsx` come from Task 3. This commit only adds buddy.ts, buddy-view.ts and its test, BuddyPanel.tsx, and edits buddy.tsx.
  
  **Quality: Needs fixes**
  
  **Critical:** None.
  
  **Important**
  1. **Failed accept or decline loses the invite** (BuddyPanel.tsx:89-96). `answer` sets `handled`, clears the stored code and hides the card even when the call failed. A network blip on Accept means the invite is gone until restart. Fix: have `act` return a boolean and only clear on success or a 400.
  2. **Any preview error clears the remembered code** (lines 60-63). One flaky request right after sign-in erases the invite. Fix: clear only on a 400 and show "could not reach" otherwise.
  3. **Nudge button state is wrong after a nudge** (BuddyPanel.tsx:152-157, buddy-view.ts:26).
     - The status API does not return `nudgedToday` (the streaks service drops it), so `canNudge` is only `!buddyLoggedToday`.
     - After a successful nudge the button stays and nothing confirms it. Tapping again shows "You can nudge once per day", and that text appears for any failure, including offline.
     - Fix: return `nudgedToday` from `getStatus`, add it to `canNudge`, and show "Nudge sent".
  4. **`acked` is never reset** (lines 35, 43-46). A second nudge in the same open session is never shown. If `ackNudge` throws, it is never retried and is misreported as "Could not reach Hissa".
  5. **Polling hygiene** (lines 38-52, 74).
     - There is no in-flight guard, so slow requests pile up and a stale response can overwrite a newer status.
     - There is no alive flag, so state updates can run after blur or unmount. The invite lookup on focus is not cancelled either.
     - Fix: a `busy` ref and an `alive` flag set false in the focus cleanup.
  6. **Stale offline message** (lines 48-49). "Could not reach Hissa. Retrying." stays on screen after a later refresh succeeds.
  
  **Minor**
  - Buttons have no in-flight disable, so a double tap on Create invite or Accept shows an error after success.
  - End pairing has no confirmation, and it resets the streak.
  - `clearPendingInvite` can throw before `setInvite(null)` runs, leaving the card up.
  - A failed route-param code is re-previewed on every focus, so the error returns each time.
  - `accessibilityLiveRegion` is Android-only. Add `accessibilityRole="alert"` for iOS.
  - The poll keeps running while the app is backgrounded.
  - invite/[code].tsx silently rejects lowercase codes.
  - Tests cover only the pure view logic, not the invite or error states.
  
  No over-engineering found. 401 handling is fine: `apiAuthed` signs the user out, the route guard leaves the tabs, and the interval is cleaned up.
</agent-message>

The full review is at /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sobanahmad-Work-8x-hackathon-hissa/1552c292-3466-48ec-bbc1-536015265170/scratchpad/review-task5.md.
  
  **What I ran**
  - In the worktree, all five commands passed: backend test (207 passed, 8 skipped), backend lint, frontend test (14 passed), frontend lint, frontend build.
  - The opt-in integration test `FEEL_LOCAL_DB_TEST=1 npm run test -w backend -- feel.repository.integration` passed against the PostgREST fixture. It covers:
    - the 89/90-minute and 24h/24h+1s boundaries;
    - a predicted-only dish being excluded;
    - another user's meal being refused (404 in the service, FK error 23503 on a direct insert);
    - the symptom CHECK constraint (23514);
    - two concurrent submits giving exactly one success and one 400;
    - a skipped symptom stored as null.
  - A standalone Express check confirmed `res.json(null)` returns 200 with body `null`.
  - I did not apply the migration twice (repeatability is from reading only), run on a phone, or run the brief's controlled-clock demo.
  
  **Spec compliance: PASS** (worktree paths, relative to `.worktrees/v2-feel`)
  - `feel.service.ts:8-10,35` enforces the 90-minute minimum and 24-hour maximum. `feel.repository.ts:60` plus the unique `meal_id` give one response per meal, including under concurrency.
  - Every query is scoped to the authenticated user: `listCandidates` and `findMeal` use `.eq('user_id')`, and `insertCheckin` takes the user only from `req.userId`. The router is mounted behind `requireAuth` (`app.ts:24-25`).
  - The composite FK in `migration:10-17` blocks attaching a check-in to another owner's meal, even for service-role inserts.
  - The due summary lists only confirmed catalog dishes and is returned only to the meal's owner.
  - The body schema is `.strict()` with enums, and the service re-validates it. A skipped symptom is stored as null. Skip in the UI writes nothing.
  - The migration is additive and repeatable (`if not exists`, guarded `do` block) and drops nothing. Its `add constraint` would fail loudly, not change data, if a legacy row had a mismatched owner.
  - The symptom row appears only after a heavy stomach, and Save does nothing without both energy and stomach.
  - The controller returns JSON `null` when nothing is due, and the client handles it.
  
  **Important**
  1. Direct client writes bypass the check-in rules: `database/supabase/migrations/20261030000000_initial_schema.sql:112` leaves a `for all to authenticated` policy on `feel_checkins`.
     - Any signed-in user can insert a check-in for their own meal at minute 5. They can also update answers or set the new `question_type`, `answer_id` and `question_source` columns to arbitrary text.
     - That feeds fake evidence to the later pattern tasks.
     - Smallest fix: add `revoke insert, update, delete on public.feel_checkins from anon, authenticated;` to the new migration. Migration `20261102000200` did the same for `buddy_pairs`.
  2. The baseline is 3 taps, not 2 (`CheckinPanel.tsx:128-134`): energy, stomach, then an explicit Save.
     - Smallest fix: auto-submit on the second answer unless the stomach is heavy. For heavy, keep the symptom row and submit on a symptom tap or a "Done" button.
     - The cost is losing the chance to correct a mis-tap. If "two answer taps plus a confirm" is acceptable, treat this as resolved.
  
  **Minor** (details are in the file)
  - A failed save (400 because the meal expired or was answered on another device) leaves the stale card on screen. Fix: call `load()` in the catch (`CheckinPanel.tsx:79`).
  - There is no in-flight guard on Save, so a double tap overwrites "Saved" with an error.
  - `message` is never cleared, so "Thanks. Saved." replaces "Nothing to check in" for good and shows on later cards.
  - Skip stores one meal id and `getDue` returns the oldest meal, so a skipped breakfast hides a due lunch until restart.
  - A meal with no confirmed dishes is still due and shows "How did this meal sit?" with nothing after it.
  - `feel.repository.ts:60` throws `ValidationError` from the repository, against the layering rule that repositories hold no business rules.
  - Save has no disabled or accessibility state before both answers are chosen.
  - Only the two pure helpers are tested; there is no component test.
  
  **Over-engineering:** none worth flagging.

The panel logic itself has no unit test, because the repo has no React Native test environment. I verified it with lint, tsc and the export bundle, not by running it on a device.
  
  Fixed:
  - **Important 1-6:**
    - `act` now returns true when the call worked or the server answered 400/404/409. Only then does the app clear the stored code and hide the invite card.

    - `getStatus` now returns `nudgedToday`, which is false unless the pair is active. The backend type, the exact-keys test (plus one new test for this field), the frontend `BuddyStatus` type, `buddyView` and `docs/architecture.md` are updated. `canNudge` is false when `nudgedToday` is true, and a "Nudge sent" label replaces the button. `backend/README.md` doesn't document the status shape, so I left it alone.

    - `busy` and `alive` refs stop overlapping polls and gate setState after an await, including the invite lookup.
    - The "Could not reach Hissa. Retrying." message clears after a later successful refresh.

  Not fixed, as you told me to skip them:
  - End-pairing confirmation.
  - Background-poll pause.
  - Lowercase invite code redirect in `invite/[code].tsx`: this was already accepted as low risk.

  Task 6 meets the spec and is approved, but one Important issue should be fixed before Task 7 wires the ranker into the API. The full review is at /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sobanahmad-Work-8x-hackathon-hissa/1552c292-3466-48ec-bbc1-536015265170/scratchpad/review-task6.md. Paths below are under `backend/`.
  
  **What I ran:**
  - `npm test`: backend 225 passed, 10 skipped; frontend 24 passed.
  - `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: both exit 0. dist has no `scripts/`, and `question-model.js` loads from dist (`useTrainedRanker` is true). I did not boot the full server, because it needs Supabase env vars.
  - Not run: `train:questions` and the opt-in DB integration test.
  - Committed `question-weights.ts` sha256 matches the claim (d8efbf5e…56f1) and is unchanged after my checks.
  - A read-only scratch script (/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sobanahmad-Work-8x-hackathon-hissa/1552c292-3466-48ec-bbc1-536015265170/scratchpad/t6-check.mts) recomputes held-out baseline 0.664 and trained 0.782, an exact match.
  
  **Spec compliance: PASS**
  - `question-model.ts:40-56`:
    - One mixed meal is a single whole-meal observation and names no dish.
    - A dish in every meal (no comparison meals) is never a candidate.
    - Beta(1,1) smoothing `(k+1)/(n+2)` is correct, and the comparison rate uses total heavy minus exposed heavy.
    - Ties are deterministic: dishes by sorted slug plus strict `>`, intents by a stable sort that keeps `symptom_detail` first.
  - `question-model.ts:85`: fewer than 2 observations always gives the generic order.
  - `feel.repository.ts:79-90`:
    - The only query path filters on `user_id`, and the composite FK keeps check-ins on their meal's owner.
    - Only confirmed dishes are used, deduplicated by slug; the 24 h cut-off includes exactly 24 h.
    - The embed names `feel_checkins_meal_id_fkey`, which separates it from the second FK.
    - `meal_id` is unique, so PostgREST returns an object, and `!inner` removes the null case. Only the opt-in integration test proves that shape.
  - `train-question-ranker.ts:13-25`:
    - Training seeds 1-2000 and held-out seeds 100001-100500 are disjoint.
    - Features come only from the observations, never from the simulator's hidden kind, and the run is deterministic.
  - Synthetic code stays out of runtime: nothing in `src/` outside `__tests__` imports `scripts/`.
  - `question-model.ts:32`: `useTrainedRanker` is derived from the training report, not hard-coded.
  - No causal or clinical wording, only disclaimers.
  - Framing of the result is fair. The baseline is simply the majority class (2/3), and the score measures recovering the simulator's label, not question relevance. Dish-linked recall is 90/168.
  
  **Quality: Approved with fixes (one Important)**
  
  **Critical:** None.
  
  **Important:**
  - `question-model.ts:84-89`: `pre_existing` (the dish question) is ranked first even when there is no dish to ask about.
    - Scenario: 10 meals of daal+roti, all fine, so there is no candidate. The score is 0.324 − 2.376/12 = 0.126 > 0, so the order is `['pre_existing', 'symptom_detail']`. I reproduced this in the scratch script.
    - Task 7 would then send Gemini a null candidate. `buildEvidence` can also return a candidate with gap ≤ 0 (30 of 500 held-out users).
    - Fix: `if (e.n < 2 || !e.candidate || e.candidate.gap <= 0) return [...GENERIC_ORDER];` plus one test. Held-out accuracy is unchanged (0 of 121 `pre_existing` picks lacked a positive-gap candidate), so no retrain is needed.
  
  **Minor:**
  - `feel.repository.ts:78-79,90`: yes, the 24 h attribution rule is a business rule in the repository, which breaks AGENTS.md rule 4. Smallest fix: expose `checkedInAt` on `MealObservation` and filter in the service in Task 7's `getQuestion`, moving the boundary test with it.
  - `feel.repository.ts:91-97`: meals with no confirmed dish still become observations and inflate every dish's comparison count. Fix: `.filter(o => o.dishes.length > 0)`.
  - `train-question-ranker.ts:72`: the output path depends on the working directory, so running the script from the repo root fails. Fix: build the path with `path.join(__dirname, '../src/modules/feel/question-weights.ts')`; the package is CJS.
  - `__tests__/question-model.test.ts`: no test pins dish tie-breaking. The synthetic-isolation test only re-checks the generator's own labels, so drop or rename it.
  - `feel.repository.ts:79-87`: the whole history is read on every call. Fine for now; add a `ponytail:` note or a window when Tasks 7 and 8 call it per request.
</agent-message>

  Re-review done. Full write-up: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sobanahmad-Work-8x-hackathon-hissa/1552c292-3466-48ec-bbc1-536015265170/scratchpad/rereview-task4-5.md
  
  Task 4: Approved. Task 5: Approved. Neither fix introduced a Critical or Important defect.
  
  **Verification run (HEAD 3751442)**
  - `npm test -w frontend && npm run lint && npm run build` exited 0.
  - `HISSA_TEST_POSTGRES_CONTAINER=hissa-claude-t3 npm test -w backend`: 233 passed, 1 skipped. The skipped file is the opt-in `feel.repository.integration`; I did not run it.
  - `feel.database.test.ts` run on its own against the fixture: 3/3 pass. Direct authenticated insert, update and delete return "permission denied", and the service role can insert.
  - Not run: the migration applied twice (checked by reading only), any device run, and component tests (none exist).
  
  **Task 4 verdicts**
  - I1, accept/decline drops the invite on failure: ADDRESSED (`BuddyPanel.tsx:126-153`).
  - I2, `openInvite` clears the code on any error: ADDRESSED (`:97-104`; only 400/404 clear it).
  - I3, no `nudgedToday`: ADDRESSED (`streaks.service.ts:64`, `buddy-view.ts:27-28`, "Nudge sent" label, docs and tests updated).
  - I4, `acked` never reset: ADDRESSED (`:74-81`).
  - I5, overlapping polls and setState after blur: ADDRESSED (`busy`/`alive`; `busy` resets in `finally`).
  - I6, stale offline message: ADDRESSED (`:72`).
  - In-flight button disable: ADDRESSED.
  - `setInvite`/`clearPendingInvite` order: ADDRESSED, though a throw from `clearPendingInvite` is still an unhandled rejection.
  - Failed link code reshown on every focus: ADDRESSED (`failed` set).
  - `accessibilityRole="alert"`: ADDRESSED.
  - End pairing confirmation: NOT ADDRESSED (optional).
  - Poll while backgrounded: NOT ADDRESSED (optional).
  - Tests for error and invite states: PARTIAL (`httpStatus` and nudge tests added, no component tests).
  
  **Task 5 verdicts**
  - I1, direct client writes bypass the window: ADDRESSED (migration `:19-25`, DB-verified).
  - I2, three taps: ADDRESSED. The non-heavy path is 2 taps. The heavy path is energy, heavy, optional symptom, then Save.
  - m1, stale due after a failed save: ADDRESSED (`CheckinPanel.tsx:90`).
  - m2, double-tap race: ADDRESSED (`saving` ref).
  - m3, message never cleared: ADDRESSED.
  - m4, skip hides the next due meal: ADDRESSED (`?skip=`).
  - m5, empty dishes: ADDRESSED (service filter).
  - m6, `ValidationError` thrown from the repository: ADDRESSED (service maps 23505, two tests).
  - m7, Save disabled state: ADDRESSED.
  - m8, panel test: NOT ADDRESSED (optional).
  
  **Specific checks**
  - **(a) `?skip=`**: the ids are used only in an in-memory `skip.includes(m.id)` after the owner-scoped query. They never reach PostgREST, so there is no injection path and no cross-user effect. Repeated or bracketed `skip` params are ignored. Ids are unvalidated, but size is bounded by Node's ~16 KB header limit. The client joins server-issued UUIDs without encoding, which is safe.
  - **(b) Migration `20261102000400`**: repeatable whether or not the old policy exists, with no data touched. The owner SELECT policy is correct. One caveat: the migration is edited in place. It is not on main, but any database that already applied the earlier version (for example other fixtures) will not pick up the change and would need a new migration.
  - **(c) `shouldAutoSubmit`**: equals both answers set and stomach not heavy, and is unit tested. A double submit is blocked by the `saving` ref plus the unique constraint, which returns 400. A mis-tap on the second answer saves with no undo. That is the trade-off the brief accepts for two taps.
  - **(d) Task 4**: `nudgedToday` is only the caller's own sent state and is false unless the pair is active. It leaks nothing about the buddy. `httpStatus` matches the exact `apiAuthed` error format. The invite survives network failure and is not re-offered after an answer. `busy` cannot stick after a thrown error, because it resets in `finally`.
  - **(e) Layering**: the repository no longer holds the rule. The service maps the DB error code, the same pattern as `streaks.service`.
  
  **New issues**
  - Critical: none.
  - Important: none.
  - Minor, with the smallest fix for each:
    1. **Stale answers on Skip** (`CheckinPanel.tsx:157-160`, and after a failed save). Skip and a failed save leave the old energy and stomach selected. If energy "good" was tapped and the user then skips, the next meal shows "good" preselected, and one stomach tap auto-submits it for the new meal. Fix: clear energy, stomach and symptom in the Skip handler.
    2. **No fetch timeout** (shared `client.ts`; `BuddyPanel.tsx:66,127`). A request that never settles leaves `busy`, `acting` or `saving` stuck, which stops polling and disables buttons. Fix: add `AbortSignal.timeout(15000)` in `apiAuthed`.
    3. **Invite card reappears** (`BuddyPanel.tsx:151`). If the user switches tab while Accept is in flight, `setInvite(null)` is skipped because `alive` is false, so the card returns. Fix: call `setInvite(null)` unconditionally.
    4. **Stale view after an action** (`BuddyPanel.tsx`, `refresh`). `refresh()` inside `act` is skipped while a poll is in flight, so the view can be stale for up to 5 seconds. The code already carries a comment acknowledging this.
    5. **Retry not discoverable** (`CheckinPanel.tsx:61,146-153`). After a failed non-heavy auto-save there is no Save button, and a follow-up load failure overwrites the save error. Fix: show Save when a save error is present.
    6. **Heavy path tap count.** The heavy path takes 3 or 4 taps; auto-submitting on the symptom tap would fit the brief's "at most one extra tap".
    7. **`skip` unvalidated.** Harmless; optionally validate it with the existing `validate('query', ...)`.
    8. **No retry control for stored-link previews.** After a failed preview of a stored invite link there is no retry control, so it retries only on the next focus.
  
  No over-engineering found.
</agent-message>

- **Authorized redirect URI:** `https://zvhwzekbgmuaaiepkcwn.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`. No JavaScript origins are needed.
- Copy the **Client ID** and **Client secret**.

**3. Supabase dashboard: three settings to change.**
- **Enable the provider:** https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/zvhwzekbgmuaaiepkcwn/auth/providers. Open Google, switch it on, and paste the Client ID and secret. Enter the secret yourself in the dashboard rather than sending it to me.
- **Redirect allow-list:** https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/zvhwzekbgmuaaiepkcwn/auth/url-configuration. Add `hissa://**`. For Expo Go, also add `exp://**`. I haven't tested that exact pattern.
- **Site URL:** it's `http://localhost:3000` now, which is fine for local use.
- Reference: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/social-login/auth-google

**One caveat about the app code.** `frontend/src/shared/auth/google.ts` hard-codes the sign-in return address as `hissa://`.
- **iOS:** it should work, even in Expo Go, because the iOS sign-in session catches that return address itself. That is my reading of how it works, not something I've tested.
- **Android in Expo Go:** it won't work. That needs the app to build its return address from Expo's helper, a small code change, and I'll make it if you want.
- **Dev builds:** `hissa://` is fine.

Once the connection is up and you've done step 3, I can read back the auth settings through the connection to confirm they're right, before you try to sign in. I can also apply the redirect list and site URL myself, but I'll ask you first because it changes the remote project's settings.

The Android redirect and the secret file are fixed. The remote migrations were blocked and need your decision, and the Supabase connection needs one more step from you.

**Android Expo Go redirect (fixed, `24196b7`):**
- **The change:** the app now builds its sign-in return address with `Linking.createURL('')`. That gives `hissa://` in a dev or production build and an `exp://…/--/` address in Expo Go.
- **Callback check:** the callback must come back to that exact address before the code is used. A callback to any other scheme is rejected, and I added a test for that.
- **Checks:** 34 frontend tests, lint and tsc pass. I haven't tried it on a phone, and I couldn't confirm that Supabase accepts the `exp://` address without a real login. Your `exp://**` allow-list entry should cover it.

**Secret file (fixed):** I moved `client_secret_…json` to `~/.config/hissa/`, readable only by you. It's no longer in the repo folder, and I never read its contents.

The screenshot is from your partner's iPhone, and the bug was real. The sign-in itself worked, but the return trip to the app failed. The fix is pushed to `main` (`cb1d734`).

**What happened**
- **Sign-in succeeded:** the Supabase logs show a Google sign-in from an iPhone at 6:39 PM, which created a user. The callback was answered normally.
- **What the phone asked for:** it asked Supabase to return to `exp://192.168.1.5:8081`, which is a Wi-Fi IP address.
- **Why that failed:** Supabase rejects any return address whose host is a bare IP, even though your `exp://**` allow-list entry would otherwise match.
  - **Fallback:** it then sends the phone to the site URL, `http://localhost:3000`. That's the "localhost" page in the screenshot.
  - **Proof:** I tested Supabase directly without changing any settings. Hostnames such as `exp://host:8081` were accepted, and every `exp://<IP>` was rejected.

**The fix**
- **How it works:** `npm run start` and `npm run dev:app` now use a small script, `frontend/scripts/start.mjs`, which makes Expo advertise your Mac's hostname instead of its Wi-Fi IP. For your Mac that's `Sobans-MacBook-Pro.local`.
- **No settings changed:** I didn't touch any Supabase settings or any app code.
- **Verified:** Supabase accepts that hostname, Expo shows it in the QR address and its manifest, and the dev server answers on it.
- **For your partner:** they need to pull `main` and restart Expo with `npm run dev:app`. It uses their own machine's name, so nothing needs editing.

**Limits:**
- **Not tested on a phone:** I haven't checked this on a device. That final step needs a real Google sign-in from a phone.
- **Android:** Android phones may not resolve `.local` names. For Android, use a development build or an Expo tunnel.
- **Busy ports:** ports 8081 and 8082 on your Mac are held by `httpd` and `nginx`, so Expo will prompt for another port. Accept it, or pass `--port`.
- **Other finding:** Expo Go signs in with a weaker PKCE check (`plain` instead of `S256`) because it lacks the crypto function. Sign-in still works, and I noted a fix for later in the status doc.

I also discarded Expo's auto-edit to `frontend/tsconfig.json` again, since it blocks merges.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=147 session=1552c292-3466-48ec-bbc1-536015265170]
timestamp: 2026-11-02T14:44:00.262Z

You don't need to change anything in Google Console. "Web application" is the right client type for this app, and an iOS client isn't needed. The fix is in the app code and is pushed to `main` (`63ebe2b`), but I haven't tested it on a phone.

**Why it works on web and not on iPhone** (from the Supabase logs):
- **Web works.** Edge and Chrome send `redirect_to=http://localhost:8081`. Supabase accepts it because the host matches your site URL, and the sign-in completes.
- **iPhone didn't.** The partner's iPhone sends `redirect_to=exp://192.168.1.5:8081`, a bare Wi-Fi IP. Supabase rejects any return address whose host is a raw IP, so it sends the phone to the site URL, `http://localhost:3000`. That's the "Safari can't open the page" screen. Google itself succeeded each time; the callback logged a successful login.

**The fix:** on iOS the app now always returns to `hissa://`, which your allow-list already accepts. I checked the library source: the iOS sign-in sheet listens for whatever scheme it's given, so this works inside Expo Go with no IP or hostname involved. Android keeps Expo's own address. Web is unchanged.

**What your partner needs to do:**
1. Pull `main`.
2. Restart Expo with a cache clear: `npx expo start -c`. Their phone logged an earlier version of the app, so this matters.
3. Sign in with a Google account on your consent screen's test-user list.

If it still fails, the Supabase logs will show what the phone sent, and I'll read them.

**Caveats:**
- **Untested on a phone:** I verified the logic with a test, a type check, lint, an iOS bundle and the library source. The final proof needs a real Google sign-in, which I can't do.
- **Android:** an Android phone in Expo Go still hits the IP problem. It needs a hostname the phone can resolve, a development build, or an Expo tunnel.
- **Flaky tests:** two of the optional database tests share one test database and can interfere when run in parallel. One failed once, then passed alone and on a rerun, with 246 passing. It's a test-only issue, noted in the status doc.

Fixed and pushed to `main` (`8d0dd86`). The cause was a regression I introduced in my earlier iOS fix.

**What the logs showed:**
- **Server side:** everything worked on the three attempts at 14:47–14:48 UTC. The iPhone now sends `redirect_to=hissa://`, so the previous fix did reach the phone. Google logged the partner in, and the Supabase callback succeeded each time.
- **On the phone:** no token request followed, which the web flow always makes. So the app rejected the result after returning, which is why you saw the generic "Could not sign in".

**The cause:** my earlier check required the returned address to start with `hissa://`. Supabase's server rewrites a `hissa://` return address to `hissa:?code=…`, dropping the slashes. I had seen exactly that earlier, in a test that printed `'hissa:'`, and didn't connect it. So every real callback was thrown away before the sign-in code was exchanged.

**The fix:** the app now compares the scheme and host instead of the exact text. A callback to a different host on the same scheme is still rejected, and I added a test for each case. I also checked that the React Native URL parser reads `hissa:?code=abc` the same way Node does. The frontend now passes 44 tests, with types and lint clean.

**What your partner needs to do:**
1. Pull `main`.
2. Restart Expo with `npx expo start -c`.
3. Tap Continue with Google again.

**Caveat:** I still can't sign in with a Google account myself, so this is verified by the logs, the tests and the URL parser, not by a real phone. If it still fails, the Supabase logs will show where, and I can read them straight away.

The sign-in screen swallows the real error behind that one generic message, which cost us a round here. I can make it show the actual error text during development. Want that?

The "photo scans are unavailable" message was Gemini timing out, and it's fixed and pushed (`4034b37`). I couldn't see the failing request itself, though. That was on the partner's machine, and I have no access to its backend log.

**What was happening:**
- **Where it comes from:** the app shows that message only when the scan API answers with the manual-mode fallback.
- **The cause:** the API returns that fallback whenever the Gemini call fails.
  - **Timeouts:** with the same photo, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` usually answered in 1–3 seconds, but 5 of 20 calls took 15 to 72 seconds. The backend gave up at 20 seconds and had no retry for timeouts, so roughly 1 scan in 6 showed the message even though Gemini was working.
  - **Matching evidence:** I reproduced this on the latest `main` with the real key and the same request the app sends. Scans failed after exactly 20 seconds and the backend logged `TimeoutError`.
- **Partner's three failures:** the three failed scans on the partner's network (14:58–15:09 UTC) fit this pattern, and so does their later 503 retry commit, which landed right after. Their backend log would confirm it.

**What I changed:**
- **Retries:** each Gemini call now gets 10 seconds, with up to 3 attempts. That covers timeouts, 503 errors and 429 quota errors, with a longer pause for 429.
- **Model:** the default is now `gemini-flash-lite-latest`. Side by side in the same window it never exceeded 3.5 seconds.
- **App timeout:** I raised it from 30 to 45 seconds, so the backend's answer arrives before the app gives up.
- **Results:** 8 of 8 scans at a normal pace, the slowest 2.5 seconds. The partner's real aloo qeema photo came back as the nearest catalog match, `kachalo-qeema` at 0.95.
- **Docs and tests:** I added tests for each new behavior and recorded this in the status doc.

**What each of you must do:**
1. Pull `main`.
2. In your own `backend/.env`, change or delete the `GEMINI_MODEL=` line, because a value there overrides the new default. I already updated yours. The partner needs to do the same on their machine.
3. Restart the backend.

**Caveats:**
- **Quota:** the free tier has a per-minute limit per model, which recovers in seconds. Two people scanning at once on one key can still hit it. My 20-scan bursts hit it once during testing, so I used some of that quota.
- **Alias model:** `gemini-flash-lite-latest` can switch to a newer model without warning. That's fine for the hackathon, but pin a name later.
- **Not tested on a phone:** I tested the API path, not the camera in Expo Go. I also couldn't tell whether any scan happened after the pull, because none appears in the database after 15:09 UTC. If it still fails on the phone, the backend terminal will show the failure reason.

I stopped the local Supabase stack and deleted my temporary files. I also kept the stray WhatsApp photo in your repo root out of git, so it can't be committed by accident.
